## Refactoring Analysis: `apps/api/src`

### File Size Overview (files over 200 lines)

| File | Lines | Priority |
|------|-------|----------|
| `auth/auth.service.ts` | 669 | High |
| `shares/shares.service.ts` | 569 | High |
| `ipns/ipns.service.ts` | 560 | High |
| `republish/republish.service.ts` | 446 | High |
| `auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts` | 339 | Medium |
| `shares/shares.controller.ts` | 316 | Medium |
| `auth/auth.controller.ts` | 293 | Medium |
| `metrics/metrics.service.ts` | 226 | Low |
| `vault/vault.service.ts` | 220 | Low |
| `tee/tee.service.ts` | 212 | Low |

---

### 1. Duplicated Redis Client Instantiation (Critical DRY Violation)

**Files affected:**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` lines 51-57
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/services/email-otp.service.ts` lines 31-36
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts` lines 53-58
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/app.module.ts` lines 37-47 (BullMQ Redis config uses the same env vars)

All three classes instantiate `new Redis({...})` with identical configuration pulled from `ConfigService`. Each also implements `OnModuleDestroy` to call `redis.quit()`.

**Refactoring suggestion:** Create a `RedisModule` that provides a shared `REDIS_CLIENT` token via `useFactory`. Inject this single managed connection into `AuthService`, `EmailOtpService`, and `IdentityController`. This also removes the need for each class to implement `OnModuleDestroy` independently. The NestJS pattern is well-established with `@nestjs/bull` already doing this for BullMQ.

---

### 2. Duplicated Delegated Routing HTTP Logic (Major DRY/SRP Violation)

**Files affected:**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts` — `publishToDelegatedRouting()` (lines 165-241) and `resolveFromDelegatedRouting()` (lines 418-512)
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/republish/republish.service.ts` — `publishSignedRecord()` (lines 208-254)

Both services contain nearly identical exponential-backoff retry loops for HTTP calls to the delegated routing API. Both have:
- The same `for (let attempt = 0; attempt < maxRetries; attempt++)` structure
- The same 429 rate-limit handling with `Retry-After` header parsing
- The same `private delay(ms)` helper (identical implementation in both files)
- The same URL template `${delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${ipnsName}`
- The same `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` config key read independently in each constructor

The `delay()` method is copy-pasted verbatim:
```
// republish.service.ts:443 and ipns.service.ts:557
private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
}
```

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract a `DelegatedRoutingClient` service (injectable, singleton) that encapsulates:
- `publish(ipnsName: string, recordBytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void>`
- `resolve(ipnsName: string): Promise<{cid, sequenceNumber, ...} | null>`
- The retry/backoff logic
- The `delay()` helper
- The `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` config lookup

Both `IpnsService` and `RepublishService` inject this client. This reduces each service by ~150 lines and eliminates the two separate config lookups.

---

### 3. Repeated "Find Share or Throw NotFoundException" Pattern (DRY)

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts`

The same 3-line pattern appears **six times** (lines 164-167, 185-188, 225-228, 244-247, 293-296, 320-323):

```typescript
const share = await this.shareRepo.findOne({ where: { id: shareId } });
if (!share) {
  throw new NotFoundException('Share not found');
}
```

After the fetch, each method then does an ownership check that is also repeated:
- `share.sharerId !== sharerId` → `throw new ForbiddenException(...)` (appears 4 times at lines 191, 231, 299, 326)
- `share.recipientId !== recipientId` → `throw new ForbiddenException(...)` (once at line 250)

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract two private helpers:

```typescript
private async findShareOrThrow(shareId: string): Promise<Share>
private assertSharer(share: Share, userId: string): void
private assertRecipient(share: Share, userId: string): void
```

This collapses the boilerplate across all six methods.

---

### 4. AuthService Violates Single Responsibility Principle (SRP)

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` (669 lines)

`AuthService` currently handles six distinct responsibilities:
1. **Core login flow** with placeholder publicKey resolution (`login()`, `verifyCipherBoxJwt()`) — lines 63-217
2. **Token lifecycle** (refresh, logout, `refreshByToken()`) — lines 219-336
3. **Auth method management** (link/unlink JWT and wallet methods) — lines 343-556
4. **Account deletion** with IPFS unpin side-effect — lines 245-271
5. **Test-only login** with deterministic keypair generation — lines 565-668
6. **Redis nonce management** for wallet linking — lines 466-483

The constructor injects 8 dependencies (5 repositories + IPFS provider + 3 services), making it difficult to test and too broad.

**Refactoring suggestion:**
- Extract `AccountService` (or fold into `VaultService`) to handle `deleteAccount()` — this is the only method that depends on both `PinnedCidRepository` and `IpfsProvider`, which are cross-domain dependencies that do not belong in `AuthService`.
- Extract `AuthMethodService` for `getLinkedMethods()`, `linkMethod()`, `linkJwtMethod()`, `linkWalletMethod()`, `unlinkMethod()`. The nonce management for wallet linking can move here or to `SiweService`.
- Move `testLogin()` and `generateDeterministicKeypair()` to a separate `TestAuthService` that is only conditionally registered (guard with a NestJS dynamic module or `NODE_ENV` check at module level).
- `login()`, `refresh()`, `refreshByToken()`, `logout()` — keep in a leaner `AuthService`.

This eliminates the cross-domain dependency: `AuthService` should not know about `PinnedCid` or `IpfsProvider`.

---

### 5. IdentityController Violates SRP (Controller with Business Logic)

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts` (339 lines)

The controller directly accesses `userRepository` and `authMethodRepository` and contains the `findOrCreateUserByIdentifier()` private business logic method (lines 297-338). Controllers should delegate to services; this logic belongs in a service.

Additionally, the link-intent check is copy-pasted in three handlers (`googleLogin`, `verifyOtp`, `walletLogin`) at lines 98-105, 170-177, and 264-268. The pattern is identical each time.

**Refactoring suggestion:**
- Move `findOrCreateUserByIdentifier()` to `AuthService` or a new `IdentityService`. The controller then calls `identityService.findOrCreate(...)` instead of directly using repositories.
- Extract `private issueIdentityToken(intent, userId, email?): Promise<IdentityTokenResponseDto>` to consolidate the link-intent handling and JWT signing into one place, called by all three handlers.

---

### 6. Duplicate `RequestWithUser` Interface Definition

**Files affected:**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/common/types.ts` (line 7) — the canonical definition
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts` (line 35) — a private, local re-declaration

`auth.controller.ts` defines its own `interface RequestWithUser extends Request { user: User }` at line 35, using the `User` entity type, while `common/types.ts` defines it with `user: { id: string; scope?: string[] }`. The controller should simply import from `common/types.ts`.

**Refactoring suggestion:** Delete the local definition in `auth.controller.ts` and import `RequestWithUser` from `../common/types`.

---

### 7. `SharesService` Mixes Share and Invite Concerns (SRP)

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts` (569 lines)

The service has a clear internal split at the comment block at line 334:

```typescript
// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Invite link methods (Phase 15)
// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
```

The invite methods (`createInvite`, `getInviteStatus`, `getInviteForClaim`, `claimInvite`, `getInvitesForItem`, `revokeInvite`) from lines 342-568 are already a self-contained concern. There are even two separate controllers for the two domains: `SharesController` (shares) and both `ShareInvitesController` + `InvitesController` (invites).

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract `ShareInviteService` containing just the invite methods. `SharesService` shrinks to ~310 lines. `ShareInvitesController` and `InvitesController` inject `ShareInviteService` instead of `SharesService`. This matches the already-split controller structure.

---

### 8. Duplicate `publicKey` 0x-normalization

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts`

The same `startsWith('0x') ? slice(2) : key` normalization for public keys appears at lines 47-49 (`createShare()`) and 265 (`lookupUserByPublicKey()`).

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract a `normalizePublicKey(key: string): string` pure function in `common/utils.ts` (create alongside `common/types.ts`). This also documents the invariant that DB stores bare hex.

---

### 9. `AuthService.deleteAccount()` Has a Cross-Domain Dependency

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` lines 245-271

`deleteAccount()` requires `PinnedCidRepository` and `IpfsProvider` — both owned by the `vault`/`ipfs` domains. This forces `AuthService` to import from those modules and have 8 constructor parameters, which increases coupling. The IPFS unpin is a side-effect of account deletion, not a core auth concern.

**Refactoring suggestion:** Either:
- Move the deletion orchestration to `VaultService.cleanupForUser(userId)` and call it from a thin orchestrator, or
- Fire a NestJS event (`EventEmitter2`) from the auth layer (`account.deleted` event) that the vault/IPFS layer handles. This decouples auth from IPFS entirely.

---

### 10. `IpnsService.publishBatch()` Duplicates Single-Record Logic

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts` lines 87-160

`publishBatch()` is essentially a concurrent wrapper around the same `upsertFolderIpns` + `publishToDelegatedRouting` calls already made in `publishRecord()`. The inline lambda at lines 102-134 repeats the base64 decode, the upsert, and the delegated routing publish.

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract a private `processSingleRecord(userId, entry, recordType?)` helper and call it from both `publishRecord()` and the `publishBatch()` map callback. This removes ~40 lines of duplication.

---

### 11. `VaultService` Has Trivial Code Smell: `getVault` vs `findVault`

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts` lines 83-108

`getVault()` and `findVault()` are identical except one throws `NotFoundException` and the other returns null. This is a valid pattern but the `toVaultResponse()` call + `teeKeyStateService.getTeeKeysDto()` call is copy-pasted three times (lines 75, 92-93, 106-107). The pattern is:

```typescript
const teeKeys = await this.teeKeyStateService.getTeeKeysDto();
return this.toVaultResponse(vault, teeKeys);
```

**Refactoring suggestion:** Extract `private async toVaultResponseWithTeeKeys(vault: Vault): Promise<VaultResponseDto>` to inline the TEE key fetch + conversion.

---

### 12. `MetricsService` Constructor Over-Initializes (Low Priority)

**File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/metrics/metrics.service.ts` (226 lines)

The constructor at lines 51-155 instantiates all 13 Prometheus metrics inline. This is not a logic problem but makes the constructor ~100 lines long. A common NestJS pattern is to use `onModuleInit` for this or extract a `createMetrics(registry)` factory.

**Refactoring suggestion:** Move metric instantiation into a private `initializeMetrics()` method called from the constructor, keeping the constructor itself readable.

## Refactoring Analysis: `apps/web/src`

### 1. Large Files That Should Be Split

**`hooks/useFolder.ts` — 1,262 lines** — The single biggest problem file.

It contains 11 distinct async operations (createFolder, renameItem, moveItem, moveItems, deleteItem, deleteItems, addFile, addFiles, updateFile, restoreVersion, deleteVersion) plus shared setup code. All 11 operations share the same boilerplate: `setState({ isLoading: true, error: null })` at the top, `setState({ isLoading: false, error: null })` at the bottom, and a catch block with `setState({ isLoading: false, error })`. That pattern repeats 11 times.

Suggested split:
- `hooks/useFolderMutations.ts` — createFolder, rename, move, moveItems, delete, deleteItems (folder/file CRUD)
- `hooks/useFileOperations.ts` — addFile, addFiles, updateFile, restoreVersion, deleteVersion (file-specific async ops)
- `hooks/useAsyncOperation.ts` — extract the loading/error wrapper pattern into a shared utility

**`components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx` — 1,153 lines**

This component does far too much. Responsibilities include: sync polling wiring, drag-and-drop state management (7 callbacks + a counter ref + a window event effect), multi-selection state and pruning logic, 12 separate `useState` dialog states with 18 open/close callbacks, batch action logic, and the entire JSX layout. Suggested split:
- `hooks/useFileBrowserDialogs.ts` — extract the 12 dialog states (renameDialog, confirmDialog, moveDialog, detailsDialog, editorDialog, imagePreviewDialog, pdfPreviewDialog, audioPlayerDialog, videoPlayerDialog, createFolderDialog, batchDeleteDialog, batchMoveDialog) plus all open/close/confirm handlers
- `hooks/useMultiSelection.ts` — selectedIds, lastSelectedIdRef, handleSelect, handleSelectAll, clearSelection, the childIds pruning effect, selectedItems derived state
- `hooks/useExternalDragDrop.ts` — the external drag detection logic (isDraggingExternal, dragCounterRef, handleContentDragEnter/Over/Leave/Drop, the window dragend/drop/dragleave effect)
- `hooks/useSyncCallback.ts` or inline into FileBrowser — the handleSync/useSyncPolling wiring

**`hooks/useSharedNavigation.ts` — 541 lines**

This hook mixes three concerns: (1) loading received shares from the API, (2) managing a nav-stack-based folder browsing state machine, (3) downloading shared files. The download logic (`downloadSharedFileFromShare` and `downloadSharedFile`) should be a separate hook or service function since it has its own distinct error surface and reusable download-store orchestration.

**`components/file-browser/ShareDialog.tsx` — 525 lines** — Contains share creation, recipient listing, revocation with inline confirm, and an invite link tab. The recipient list + revoke logic (lines ~350-525) is independently testable and should be its own `ShareRecipientList` component.

**`services/file-metadata.service.ts` — 510 lines** — Reasonable for a service; the repetition inside it is the issue (see section 3).

---

### 2. Code Duplication

**File-type detection — exact duplication across two files:**

`FileBrowser.tsx` lines 54–184 and `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` lines 25–151 define identical (or near-identical) sets of constants and functions:
- `TEXT_EXTENSIONS` (Set) — duplicated
- `TEXT_FILENAMES` (Set) — duplicated
- `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`, `PDF_EXTENSIONS`, `AUDIO_EXTENSIONS`, `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` — duplicated
- `isTextFile()`, `isImageFile()`, `isPdfFile()`, `isAudioFile()`, `isVideoFile()`, `isPreviewableFile()` — all duplicated

Note: `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` has a slightly larger `TEXT_EXTENSIONS` set (includes `.scss`, `.less`, `.mjs`, `.bat`, etc.) and its `isPreviewableFile` includes text files, but `FileBrowser.tsx` does not. The sets should be merged into a canonical `utils/fileTypes.ts` module.

**`uint8ToBase64` helper — duplicated across two services:**
- `/services/folder.service.ts` lines 38–44
- `/services/file-metadata.service.ts` lines 39–45

Identical implementation. Should live in `utils/encoding.ts` or `lib/crypto/encoding.ts`.

**`truncatePublicKey` / `truncatePubkey` — near-identical implementations:**
- `ShareDialog.tsx` line 62: `truncateKey(key)` — `0x${hex.slice(0,4)}...${hex.slice(-4)}`
- `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` line 160: `truncatePubkey(pubkey)` — identical logic, different name

Should be a single exported utility in `utils/format.ts` (which already exists for `formatBytes`/`formatDate`).

**`getRootFolderState` vs `getRootFolder` — parallel helpers with minor differences:**
- `hooks/useFolder.ts` line 38: `getRootFolderState(vaultStore, folders)` — checks vault key presence, constructs FolderNode
- `hooks/useFolderNavigation.ts` line 32: `getRootFolder(vaultStore, folders)` — reversed check order, same logic

The `parentId === 'root' ? getRootFolderState(vault, folders) : folders[parentId]` ternary appears **10 times** inside `useFolder.ts` alone. This should be extracted into a single utility function (e.g., `resolveFolderById(id, vault, folders)`) exported from `folder.service.ts` or a shared helper.

**`MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH = 20` — defined in three places:**
- `services/folder.service.ts` line 35
- `hooks/useFolder.ts` line 24
- `components/file-browser/MoveDialog.tsx` line 9

Should be a single exported constant from `folder.service.ts`.

**Inline IPFS fetch+decode pattern — 4 repetitions in `useSharedNavigation.ts`:**

```typescript
const encryptedBytes = await fetchFromIpfs(resolved.cid);
const encryptedJson = new TextDecoder().decode(encryptedBytes);
const encrypted: EncryptedFolderMetadata = JSON.parse(encryptedJson);
const metadata = await decryptFolderMetadata(encrypted, key);
```

This appears at lines 213–216, 289–292 (folder), and 413–416, 473–476 (file). The `folder.service.ts` already has `fetchAndDecryptMetadata(cid, folderKey)` for the folder case — `useSharedNavigation.ts` should use it instead of inlining. A parallel `fetchAndDecryptFileMetadata(cid, folderKey)` should be added to `file-metadata.service.ts`.

**Login flow REQUIRED_SHARE handling — triplicated across three login methods:**

In `hooks/useAuth.ts`, `loginWithGoogle` (lines 261–285), `loginWithEmail` (lines 318–332), and `loginWithWallet` (lines 358–371) each have an identical REQUIRED_SHARE block:
```typescript
if (coreKitStatus === 'required_share') {
  setPendingAuth(cipherboxJwt, '<method>');
  const tempLoginResponse = await authApi.login({ idToken: cipherboxJwt, publicKey: `pending-core-kit-${userId}`, loginType: 'corekit' });
  setAccessToken(tempLoginResponse.accessToken);
  return;
}
```

This should be extracted into a private `handleRequiredShare(cipherboxJwt, userId, method)` helper inside `useAuth`.

**Lazy IPNS key migration block — triplicated in `useFolder.ts`:**

Lines 951–966 (handleUpdateFile), 1080–1096 (handleRestoreVersion), and 1192–1215 (handleDeleteVersion) each contain the same 15-line block:
```typescript
if (migratedIpnsPrivateKeyEncrypted) {
  folderService.updateFolderMetadata({ ... }).then(({ newSequenceNumber }) => {
    useFolderStore.getState().updateFolderSequence(parentId, newSequenceNumber);
  }).catch((err) => {
    console.warn('Lazy IPNS key migration: ...');
  });
}
```

Should be extracted into a private `persistMigratedIpnsKey(parentFolder, parentId, updatedChildren, migratedKey)` helper inside `useFolder.ts`.

**Download store lifecycle sequence — duplicated in `useSharedNavigation.ts`:**

`downloadSharedFileFromShare` (lines 400, 440–445) and `downloadSharedFile` (lines 462, 498–503) each manually orchestrate `startDownload → setDecrypting → setSuccess / setError`. If a third download path is added this will diverge. Consider wrapping in a `useDownloadOperation(fileName, fn)` abstraction.

---

### 3. Components Doing Too Much (SRP violations)

**`FileBrowser.tsx`** — Already described above. Has 7+ distinct concerns in one component: sync, drag-and-drop, multi-select, 12 dialog states, batch actions, route wiring, and render. The JSX is 270 lines alone (lines 874–1153). Target: reduce to a thin coordinator that delegates to purpose-built hooks.

**`useFolder.ts`** — An over-generalized hook that acts as both a "folder CRUD" hook AND a "file lifecycle" hook. Creating a file (addFile/addFiles) involves IPNS creation, wrapping keys, share re-wrapping, and quota refresh — a very different surface from deleteItem or renameItem. The "version management" methods (restoreVersion, deleteVersion) belong in a separate `useFileVersions` hook.

**`useSharedNavigation.ts`** — Conflates navigation state management with download execution and API loading. The download functions (`downloadSharedFileFromShare`, `downloadSharedFile`) are side-effectful and long; they should live in a `useSharedFileDownload` hook or a download service function.

**`DetailsDialog.tsx` — 664 lines** — Contains a full file metadata resolver, CopyableValue utility component, version history display, version restore/delete wiring, and IPFS CID management. A `VersionHistory` sub-component (with its own version resolution logic) should be extracted.

---

### 4. Repeated Patterns That Should Be Abstracted

**`useAsyncOperation` / operation wrapper pattern:**

Every callback in `useFolder.ts` follows:
```typescript
const handleX = useCallback(async (...) => {
  setState({ isLoading: true, error: null });
  try {
    // ... 20-100 lines of domain logic
    setState({ isLoading: false, error: null });
  } catch (err) {
    const error = err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'Failed to X';
    setState({ isLoading: false, error });
    throw err;
  }
}, []);
```

This repeats **11 times**. A simple wrapper:
```typescript
async function withLoading<T>(setState: SetState, fn: () => Promise<T>, fallbackMsg: string): Promise<T>
```
would remove ~44 lines of boilerplate.

**Dialog state pattern:**

`FileBrowser.tsx` has 10 dialog states all typed as `{ open: boolean; item: FolderChild | null }` with corresponding open/close handlers. The 18 callbacks for opening/closing dialogs (lines 799–846) are all one-liners that just call `setXDialog({ open: bool, item: null/item })`. A `useDialogState<T>()` hook returning `[state, open, close]` would collapse this to:
```typescript
const [renameDialog, openRename, closeRename] = useDialogState<FolderChild>();
const [moveDialog, openMove, closeMove] = useDialogState<FolderChild>();
// ...
```

**`resolveFolderById` pattern:**

`parentId === 'root' ? getRootFolderState(vault, folders) : folders[parentId]` appears 10 times in `useFolder.ts`. Extract to:
```typescript
function resolveFolderById(id: string, vault: VaultState, folders: Record<string, FolderNode>): FolderNode | null
```

**File pointer lookup pattern:**

```typescript
const filePointer = parentFolder.children.find(c => c.type === 'file' && c.id === fileId) as FilePointer | undefined;
if (!filePointer) throw new Error('File not found in folder');
```
Appears 3 times in `useFolder.ts`. Extract to a typed helper `findFilePointer(parentFolder, fileId): FilePointer`.

---

### 5. State Management Patterns That Could Be Simplified

**Folder store breadcrumb duplication:**

`useFolderNavigation.ts` computes `breadcrumbs` with `useMemo`, then syncs them to the folder store via `useEffect` (lines 146–149). The folder store's `breadcrumbs` field is then apparently unused in the rest of the app (no component reads `useFolderStore(s => s.breadcrumbs)` directly for rendering). The store breadcrumb field is redundant state — it duplicates what the hook already exposes. Either the hook should be the single source of truth, or the store should compute it.

**`useSharedNavigation` local state vs store:**

The hook maintains its own `sharedItems`, `folderChildren`, `folderKey`, `breadcrumbs`, `isLoading`, `error` all as `useState`. The `useShareStore` is also used (`setReceivedShares`, `removeReceivedShare`). There is dual state: local and store. The local state duplicates what's in the store. Consider lifting shared items fully into `share.store.ts` and deriving from it, removing the parallel `sharedItems` local state.

**Dialog state as 12 separate `useState` calls:**

In `FileBrowser.tsx`, 12 dialog states could be managed as a single discriminated union:
```typescript
type ActiveDialog =
  | { type: 'rename'; item: FolderChild }
  | { type: 'delete'; item: FolderChild }
  | { type: 'move'; item: FolderChild }
  // ...
  | null;
```
This also makes it structurally impossible to have two dialogs open simultaneously (which is the intended behavior).

**`pendingPublishes: Set<string>` in folder store:**

`addPendingPublish` and `removePendingPublish` are defined in the store but appear to not be called anywhere in the current codebase. Verify and remove if unused.

---

## Refactoring Opportunities Report

### 1. Large Rust Files (>300 lines) — Split Candidates

**Priority: High**

| File | Lines | Problem |
|------|-------|---------|
| `fuse/operations.rs` | 2,602 | All 15+ FUSE callbacks in one file |
| `fuse/windows/operations.rs` | 2,644 | Same, for WinFsp backend |
| `fuse/mod.rs` | 1,246 | Mixes mount logic, publish machinery, and prepopulate |
| `crypto/tests.rs` | 1,201 | Monolithic test file |
| `fuse/inode.rs` | 937 | Inode table + type defs + `populate_folder` all together |
| `commands.rs` | 907 | All Tauri IPC commands in one file |

**Concrete split suggestions:**

**`fuse/operations.rs` (2,602 lines) and `fuse/windows/operations.rs` (2,644 lines)**

Both files have exactly the same set of 37 function definitions. They can each be split along logical groups that mirror FUSE/WinFsp operation categories:

- `fuse/operations/read_ops.rs` — `lookup`, `getattr`, `readdir`, `read`, `open`, `release` (read-only path)
- `fuse/operations/write_ops.rs` — `create`, `write`, `release` (dirty), `setattr`, `flush`
- `fuse/operations/dir_ops.rs` — `mkdir`, `rmdir`, `rename`, `unlink`
- `fuse/operations/helpers.rs` — `fetch_and_populate_folder`, `fetch_and_decrypt_content_async`, `publish_file_metadata`, `mime_from_extension`, `is_platform_special`

**`fuse/mod.rs` (1,246 lines)**

Split into:
- `fuse/mount.rs` — `mount_filesystem`, `unmount_filesystem`, `mount_point`, `pre_populate_root`
- `fuse/publish.rs` — `PublishCoordinator`, `spawn_metadata_publish`, `flush_publish_queue`, debounce queue

**`commands.rs` (907 lines)**

Split into:
- `commands/auth.rs` — `handle_auth_complete`, `handle_session_restore`, `try_silent_refresh`, `complete_auth_setup`, `logout`, helper functions (`extract_user_id_from_jwt`, `derive_public_key`)
- `commands/vault.rs` — `initialize_vault`, `fetch_and_decrypt_vault`
- `commands/sync.rs` — `start_sync_daemon`
- `commands/debug.rs` — `handle_test_login_complete`, `log_js_error`, `get_dev_key` (all `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`)
- `commands/oauth.rs` — `open_oauth_popup`

---

### 2. Duplicated Code Within the Rust Desktop App

**Priority: High — these are exact duplicates between macOS and Windows backends**

**`fetch_and_decrypt_content_async` — exact duplicate**

Found in both:
- `/apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/operations.rs` lines 396–432
- `/apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/operations.rs` lines 329–376

The function body is byte-for-byte identical: fetches from IPFS, ECIES-unwraps the file key, dispatches on `"CTR"` vs `"GCM"` mode, decrypts. This should live in `fuse/decrypt.rs` (which already exists for platform-agnostic decrypt logic) and be `pub` so both backends call it.

**`publish_file_metadata` — near-exact duplicate**

Found in both:
- `fuse/operations.rs` lines 438–~530
- `fuse/windows/operations.rs` lines 378–~460

Identical: encrypts `FileMetadata`, packages as JSON `{ iv, data }` envelope, uploads to IPFS, resolves IPNS sequence, creates IPNS record, publishes.

**`fetch_and_populate_folder` — near-exact duplicate**

Found in both:
- `fuse/operations.rs` lines 208–248
- `fuse/windows/operations.rs` lines 528–573

The Windows version calls `crate::fuse::block_with_timeout` instead of the module-local one, but otherwise identical.

**`resolve_file_pointers_blocking` — near-exact duplicate**

Found in both:
- `fuse/operations.rs` lines 254–~310
- `fuse/windows/operations.rs` lines 576–~630

**`mime_from_extension` — exact duplicate**

Found in both:
- `fuse/operations.rs` lines 87–126
- `fuse/windows/operations.rs` lines 220–265

Same 35 MIME type mappings, same function signature, exact same code. This should be moved to a shared `fuse/helpers.rs` or into `fuse/mod.rs`.

**Constants duplicated verbatim across both backends:**

```
QUOTA_BYTES: u64 = 500 * 1024 * 1024      // operations.rs:50, windows/operations.rs:41
MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE: usize = 10          // operations.rs:53, windows/operations.rs:87
VERSION_COOLDOWN_MS: u64 = 15 * 60 * 1000 // operations.rs:57, windows/operations.rs:90
CONTENT_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT: Duration::from_secs(120) // operations.rs:138, windows/operations.rs:93
```

These should be `pub const` in `fuse/mod.rs` or a dedicated `fuse/constants.rs`.

**`decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs` — three-way duplication**

The same JSON-parse + hex-decode-IV + base64-decode-data + reconstruct-sealed-blob + call-`decrypt_folder_metadata` logic appears in:
1. `fuse/operations.rs` lines 168–200 (as a private inner function inside the `implementation` module)
2. `fuse/windows/mod.rs` lines 24–64 (as a private `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs` in `mount_impl`)
3. `fuse/decrypt.rs` lines 5–44 (as `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public`)

The `fuse/decrypt.rs` version already exists as a shared extract. The fix is to delete the two private duplicates in `operations.rs` and `windows/mod.rs` and use `crate::fuse::decrypt::decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public` from both.

**`decode private key from hex` — duplicated three times in `commands.rs`**

Lines 42–51, 286–295, and 517–526 all contain:
```rust
let private_key_hex = if private_key.starts_with("0x") { &private_key[2..] } else { &private_key };
let private_key_bytes = hex::decode(private_key_hex).map_err(...)?;
if private_key_bytes.len() != 32 { return Err(...); }
```
This should be a private helper function `fn parse_private_key_hex(hex: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>`.

---

### 3. Crypto Code: Rust Desktop vs TypeScript `packages/crypto`

**Assessment: intentional parallel implementation, but alignment can be improved**

The crypto algorithms are correctly parallelized (Rust on desktop, Web Crypto API on web). This is by design — Rust runs native crypto without WASM overhead, TypeScript uses Web Crypto. The key concern is divergence.

**Where they align well:**
- AES-256-GCM sealed format (`IV || ciphertext || tag`) — `crypto/aes.rs` vs `packages/crypto/src/aes/`
- ECIES wrapping using compatible libraries (`ecies` Rust crate vs `eciesjs` npm, same author) — `crypto/ecies.rs` vs `packages/crypto/src/ecies/`
- HKDF domain separation strings identical: `"CipherBox-v1"` salt, `"cipherbox-vault-ipns-v1"` info — `crypto/hkdf.rs` vs `packages/crypto/src/keys/hierarchy.ts`
- `FolderMetadata` JSON schema (camelCase, `v2` version string) — `crypto/folder.rs` vs `packages/crypto/src/folder/types.ts`

**Where there is risk of divergence:**

The IPNS record creation is implemented from scratch in Rust (`crypto/ipns.rs`, 408 lines including manual CBOR encoding, protobuf marshaling, and base36 CID derivation), while the TypeScript version delegates to the `ipns` and `@libp2p/crypto` npm packages. Both produce compatible output (verified by the `crypto/tests.rs` test suite), but the Rust implementation is a hand-rolled reimplementation of library behavior. If the IPNS spec or `ipns` package changes format, the Rust version will silently diverge.

**Recommendation:** Add a cross-platform test vector in `crypto/tests.rs` that verifies the Rust-generated IPNS record can be parsed by the TypeScript `ipns` package, run as part of CI. This is already partially done but should be formalized.

---

### 4. Shared Packages (`packages/`) — What Is Missing

**`packages/api-client`** exists as a directory but contains only `openapi.json` — no source code. The generated TypeScript client lives inside `apps/web/src/api/` (generated by `pnpm api:generate`). The desktop app has its own hand-written Rust HTTP client in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/api/`.

**Opportunity: extract `packages/types`**

The API types that both apps share are currently:
- Generated TypeScript DTOs in `apps/web/src/api/models/` (e.g., `VaultResponse`, `InitVaultRequest`, `LoginResponse`)
- Hand-written Rust structs in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/api/types.rs` (same shape)

There is no canonical type definition. When the API changes a field name (e.g., in a PR touching `apps/api`), someone must remember to update both the generated TS client AND the Rust `types.rs` manually. The `pnpm api:generate` script handles the TS side but has no Rust counterpart.

**Concrete suggestion:** Add a `rust-codegen` step to `pnpm api:generate` that generates `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/api/types.rs` from `openapi.json` using `openapi-generator` or `oapi-codegen`. Until then, add a comment to `api/types.rs` pointing to the OpenAPI spec fields it mirrors, and add a lint check that verifies field names match.

---

### 5. Monorepo DRY Violations

**ESLint/TypeScript config duplication**

`apps/web`, `apps/desktop` (frontend), and `packages/crypto` each maintain their own `tsconfig.json` with overlapping compiler options. The root `tsconfig.base.json` exists but is not consistently extended across all packages. `apps/api` uses a separate Jest-based TypeScript setup instead of Vitest.

**QUOTA constant**

`500 * 1024 * 1024` bytes (500 MiB storage quota) appears in:
- `fuse/operations.rs:50` (macOS constant)
- `fuse/windows/operations.rs:41` (Windows constant)
- `apps/web/src/stores/quota.store.ts` (if the limit is also enforced client-side — worth checking)

This should be defined once and referenced from a shared location.

**API base URL handling**

Both apps have their own `VITE_API_URL` / `CIPHERBOX_API_URL` env var logic with slightly different fallback defaults:
- Web: `import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || 'http://localhost:3000'` (`apps/web/src/lib/api/client.ts:14`)
- Desktop frontend: same Vite env var
- Desktop Rust backend: reads `CIPHERBOX_API_URL` env var in `main.rs`

---

### 6. E2E Test Utility Patterns

**`utils/api-helpers.ts` is a stub with TODOs**

`/tests/e2e/utils/api-helpers.ts` contains three placeholder functions — `getAuthToken`, `cleanVault`, `seedTestFiles` — all marked `TODO` and throwing/warning. The actual test setup logic is inline in `utils/web3auth-helpers.ts`. The intended split (auth logic vs API setup logic) was never completed. The vault init/cleanup code in `loginViaTestEndpoint` should be refactored into the api-helpers file.

**`injectAuthState` is called from multiple places**

`web3auth-helpers.ts` exports `injectAuthState` and it is called directly in tests. This is good, but the `page.evaluate` type casting inside (`__ZUSTAND_STORES`) is fragile and duplicated if any test needs to inject partial state. A typed wrapper that knows the Zustand store shape would be safer.

**`reinjectTestAuthAfterReload` is only sometimes needed**

Several test files likely call `page.reload()` and then conditionally call `reinjectTestAuthAfterReload`. There's no shared `beforeEach`/`afterEach` pattern for this — each test that reloads must remember to call it. A base test fixture that wraps the Playwright `test` function with automatic state re-injection would eliminate this repetition.

---

### Summary: Priority-Ordered Action Items

1. **Extract duplicate helper functions** out of `fuse/operations.rs` and `fuse/windows/operations.rs` into `fuse/helpers.rs`: `mime_from_extension`, `fetch_and_decrypt_content_async`, `publish_file_metadata`, `fetch_and_populate_folder`, `resolve_file_pointers_blocking`. Stop using the private `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs` copies and use `fuse::decrypt::decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public` everywhere.

2. **Move duplicated constants** (`QUOTA_BYTES`, `MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE`, `VERSION_COOLDOWN_MS`, `CONTENT_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT`) to `fuse/mod.rs` as `pub(crate) const`.

3. **Split `commands.rs`** into `commands/auth.rs`, `commands/vault.rs`, `commands/debug.rs`, `commands/oauth.rs`, `commands/sync.rs`.

4. **Split `fuse/operations.rs`** (and its Windows twin) into `read_ops.rs`, `write_ops.rs`, `dir_ops.rs`, `helpers.rs`.

5. **Extract `parse_private_key_hex`** private helper in `commands.rs` to eliminate the three-way duplication.

6. **Complete `tests/e2e/utils/api-helpers.ts`** by moving vault init/cleanup logic from `web3auth-helpers.ts` and adding a base Playwright fixture for automatic auth re-injection after reloads.

7. **Add Rust type generation** to `pnpm api:generate` to keep `api/types.rs` in sync with the OpenAPI schema without manual updates.

### 2. Duplicated Code Patterns

#### A. File Type Detection Sets - Exact Duplication Between Two Components

The most clear-cut duplication in the codebase. Six helper functions and five `Set` constants are copy-pasted verbatim between `FileBrowser.tsx` and `SharedFileBrowser.tsx`:

Both files independently define:
```
const TEXT_EXTENSIONS = new Set([...])
const TEXT_FILENAMES = new Set([...])
const IMAGE_EXTENSIONS = new Set([...])
const PDF_EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.pdf'])
const AUDIO_EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.mp3', '.wav', ...])
const VIDEO_EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.mp4', ...])

function isTextFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isImageFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isPdfFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isAudioFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isVideoFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isPreviewableFile(name: string): boolean { ... }
function isFilePointer(item: FolderChild): item is FilePointer { ... }
```

Confirmed by exact grep: both files each have **5 matching constant declarations** and **7 matching function definitions**. These belong in a shared `fileTypeUtils.ts` utility.

#### B. Duplicated `FileMetadata` Type Definition

Three separate `FileMetadata` definitions exist:
- `packages/crypto/src/file/types.ts:30` - canonical definition (in the shared package)
- `apps/web/src/services/download.service.ts:10` - local `Pick<>` variant for download
- `apps/web/src/api/models/` - auto-generated from OpenAPI spec (expected)

The `download.service.ts` variant is a partial type that overlaps with but differs from the canonical one in `@cipherbox/crypto`.

#### C. Duplicated `InitVaultDto` Type

Three definitions:
- `apps/web/src/lib/api/vault.ts:23` - hand-written `type InitVaultDto = {...}`
- `apps/web/src/api/models/initVaultDto.ts:9` - auto-generated `interface InitVaultDto`
- `apps/api/src/vault/dto/init-vault.dto.ts:9` - source class (NestJS)

The hand-written one in `lib/api/vault.ts` should be replaced by the generated one.

#### D. Repeated `try/catch` Catch Variable Inconsistency

206 total catch blocks across the web and API source:
- 101 uses of `} catch (err) {`
- 54 uses of `} catch {` (no binding)
- 41 uses of `} catch (error) {`
- 5 uses of `} catch (e) {`
- 3 uses of `} catch (err: unknown) {`
- 1 use of `} catch (error: unknown) {`

No consistent convention. Three different variable names (`err`, `error`, `e`) are in use simultaneously.

#### E. Generated API Client Function Overloads (Expected Pattern, Not True Duplication)

The 4-count matches on `useVaultControllerGetVault` etc. are TypeScript function overloads in the auto-generated client, not true duplicates. Each generated hook has 3 overloads + 1 implementation at lines 264, 283, 302, 317 of `apps/web/src/api/vault/vault.ts`. This is expected codegen output.

---

### 3. Files with Many Responsibilities

#### Most Import-Heavy Files

| Imports | File |
|---|---|
| 39 | `apps/api/scripts/generate-openapi.ts` |
| 35 | `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx` |
| 29 | `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` |

| 12 | `apps/web/src/routes/Login.tsx` |

`generate-openapi.ts` (39 imports) is a script that manually re-imports every controller and entity to build a mock NestJS context - a known workaround but a maintenance burden every time a new module is added.

#### useFolder.ts - Wide API Surface (1,262 lines)

This single hook exposes 11 operations plus state:
```
createFolder, renameItem, moveItem, moveItems,
deleteItem, deleteItems, addFile, addFiles,
updateFile, restoreVersion, deleteVersion, clearError
```
Each of these wraps a complete async operation that interacts with IPFS, IPNS, and the folder store. Functionally it is an aggregate of what could reasonably be 4-5 separate hooks.

#### useAuth.ts - 6 Stores Coordinated in One Hook

`useAuth.ts` imports from **6 separate Zustand stores**: `auth.store`, `vault.store`, `folder.store`, `sync.store`, `device-registry.store`, `mfa.store`. It orchestrates all of them in login and logout flows. At 510 lines it is the single integration point for the entire session lifecycle.

#### FileBrowser.tsx - Cross-Domain Rendering Component

At 1,153 lines with 35 imports, it pulls from:
- 8 different hooks
- 2 services
- 10+ UI dialog components

This is the heaviest React component in the codebase.

#### `apps/desktop/src/auth.ts` - Duplicates Web Auth Logic (771 lines)

The desktop's `auth.ts` re-implements a parallel auth flow (Google OAuth, email OTP, wallet, device approval, session restore, logout) that is almost identical in shape to `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts`. These two authentication paths cannot easily share code due to the Tauri/browser split, but the structural duplication is real.

---

### 4. Services Importing Zustand Stores Directly (Architecture Pattern Risk)

**12 locations** in `apps/web/src/services/` call `useStore.getState()` directly (bypassing React's hook call convention and creating an implicit coupling layer):

| File | Store accessed |
|---|---|
| `delete.service.ts` | `useQuotaStore` |
| `device-registry.service.ts` | `useAuthStore` |
| `file-metadata.service.ts` | `useAuthStore` |
| `folder.service.ts` | `useAuthStore` (x2) |
| `invite.service.ts` | `useAuthStore` (x2) |
| `share.service.ts` | `useShareStore` (x3) |
| `upload.service.ts` | `useQuotaStore` (x2) |

This is a documented Zustand pattern (`.getState()` outside React is valid), but it does create an implicit dependency direction: services depend on stores. The stores themselves only depend on `zustand` and the crypto package, so no circular dependency exists. However, it means services cannot be used in non-Zustand contexts without side effects.

`quota.store.ts` additionally calls `vaultApi` directly from within the store definition (fetch inside `create()`), which inverts the usual direction and means the store has an active network dependency.

---

### 5. Barrel Files Assessment

37 `index.ts` barrel files found. Key ones:

| File | Lines | Character |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/crypto/src/index.ts` | 156 | Curated - selectively re-exports only safe public APIs |
| `apps/web/src/api/models/index.ts` | 109 | Auto-generated - re-exports all 60+ DTO types |
| `apps/web/src/hooks/index.ts` | 17 | Simple barrel |
| `apps/web/src/services/index.ts` | 15 | Simple barrel |
| `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/index.ts` | 19 | Simple barrel |

The crypto package barrel is notably well-structured: it explicitly chooses what to export and keeps the comment "only safe public utilities" for the utils section, intentionally not re-exporting internal helpers.

The API model barrel in `apps/web/src/api/models/index.ts` re-exports every DTO. Since these are generated files, that is expected behavior.

**Circular dependency risk:** Low. The import direction is clean:
```
components -> hooks -> services -> stores -> zustand
                    -> lib/api -> (external)
                    -> @cipherbox/crypto (package, no web deps)
```
No stores import from services or hooks. No services import from components or hooks (only from stores via `.getState()`). The one exception is the `quota.store.ts` → `vaultApi` edge, but `vaultApi` is a plain API wrapper with no React or Zustand dependencies.

---

### Summary of Key Findings

1. **Largest files needing attention:** `fuse/operations.rs` (2,644 lines) and `fuse/windows/operations.rs` (2,602 lines) are the two largest. Both implement filesystem operation callbacks for their respective OS backends - the structural parallel between them mirrors the architecture, but each is large enough to be split by operation category (read ops vs write ops).

2. **Clearest refactor opportunity:** The 7 file-type helper functions in `FileBrowser.tsx` and `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` are exact duplicates. Extracting to `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/fileTypeUtils.ts` would eliminate ~150 lines of duplication.

3. **Type definition drift:** `InitVaultDto` and `FileMetadata` each have hand-written duplicates alongside the generated/canonical versions. The hand-written ones in `lib/api/vault.ts` and `download.service.ts` should be eliminated in favor of the generated API models or the `@cipherbox/crypto` package types.

4. **`useFolder.ts` at 1,262 lines** is the largest single-responsibility-violation candidate in the TypeScript side. It handles create, rename, move, delete, add file, add files, update file, restore version, and delete version - each a separate async workflow with its own IPNS publish cycle.

5. **`generate-openapi.ts` at 39 imports** manually mirrors `app.module.ts`. Every time a new controller is added, this script must be updated manually or the OpenAPI spec silently omits the new endpoint.

6. **No true circular dependencies detected.** The layered architecture (components -> hooks -> services -> stores) is consistently maintained.

## Codebase Refactoring Analysis

**Codebase:** ~98K lines across 566 source files (31K web, 22K API, 14K desktop Rust, 8K E2E tests, 7K crypto package)

---

### Tier 1: High-Impact Quick Wins

#### 1. Extract file-type utilities (Web) — ~150 lines eliminated
`FileBrowser.tsx` and `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` have **7 identical functions** and **5 identical constant Sets** copy-pasted between them (`isTextFile`, `isImageFile`, `isPdfFile`, `isAudioFile`, `isVideoFile`, `isPreviewableFile`, `isFilePointer`, plus `TEXT_EXTENSIONS`, `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`, etc.).
- **Fix:** Extract to `utils/fileTypes.ts`

#### 2. Extract `DelegatedRoutingClient` (API) — ~300 lines consolidated
`ipns.service.ts` (560 lines) and `republish.service.ts` (446 lines) duplicate:
- Identical exponential-backoff retry loops with 429/Retry-After handling
- Identical `delay()` helper (verbatim copy-paste)
- Identical `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` config lookups
- Same URL template construction
- **Fix:** New injectable `DelegatedRoutingClient` service with `publish()` and `resolve()` methods

#### 3. Extract shared FUSE helpers (Desktop Rust) — ~500 lines eliminated
`fuse/operations.rs` (2,602 lines) and `fuse/windows/operations.rs` (2,644 lines) have **5 exact-duplicate functions:**
- `fetch_and_decrypt_content_async` — byte-for-byte identical
- `publish_file_metadata` — near-identical
- `fetch_and_populate_folder` — near-identical
- `resolve_file_pointers_blocking` — near-identical
- `mime_from_extension` — exact duplicate (35 MIME mappings)
- Plus 4 duplicated constants (`QUOTA_BYTES`, `MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE`, etc.)
- **Fix:** Move to `fuse/helpers.rs` and `fuse/constants.rs`; delete the 3 private `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs` copies in favor of the existing `fuse::decrypt::decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public`

#### 4. Extract `useDialogState` hook (Web) — simplifies `FileBrowser.tsx`
`FileBrowser.tsx` (1,153 lines) has **12 separate `useState` calls** for dialog state + **18 open/close callbacks** that are all one-liners. A simple `useDialogState<T>()` hook returning `[state, open, close]` collapses this to 12 one-liners.

---

### Tier 2: Medium-Impact Structural Splits

#### 5. Split `useFolder.ts` (1,262 lines) — 3 hooks
- `useFolderMutations` — create, rename, move, delete operations
- `useFileOperations` — addFile, addFiles, updateFile
- `useFileVersions` — restoreVersion, deleteVersion
- Plus extract `withLoading()` wrapper (the same try/catch/setState boilerplate repeats **11 times**), `resolveFolderById()` helper (repeated **10 times**), and lazy IPNS migration block (repeated **3 times**)

#### 6. Split `AuthService` (669 lines, 8 injected deps) — SRP violation
Currently handles 6 distinct responsibilities. Split into:
- `AuthService` — login, refresh, logout (core flow)
- `AuthMethodService` — link/unlink JWT and wallet methods
- `AccountService` — deleteAccount (currently drags in IPFS/Pinata deps that don't belong in auth)
- `TestAuthService` — testLogin, deterministic keypair (conditionally registered)

#### 7. Split `SharesService` (569 lines) — already has a natural seam
Comment block at line 334 marks `// Invite link methods (Phase 15)`. Controllers are already split (`SharesController` + `ShareInvitesController`), but they both inject the same monolith service.
- **Fix:** Extract `ShareInviteService` for invite methods

#### 8. Split `commands.rs` (907 lines) into modules
- `commands/auth.rs` — auth handlers + `parse_private_key_hex` (currently duplicated 3 times)
- `commands/vault.rs` — vault init/fetch
- `commands/sync.rs` — sync daemon
- `commands/debug.rs` — `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` test helpers
- `commands/oauth.rs` — OAuth popup management

#### 9. Split FUSE operations files by category
Both `fuse/operations.rs` and `fuse/windows/operations.rs` can split into:
- `read_ops.rs` — lookup, getattr, readdir, read, open
- `write_ops.rs` — create, write, setattr, flush
- `dir_ops.rs` — mkdir, rmdir, rename, unlink

---

### Tier 3: Lower-Priority Cleanup

| Issue | Location | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Redis client instantiated 3x identically | `auth.service.ts`, `email-otp.service.ts`, `identity.controller.ts` | Extract `RedisModule` with shared `REDIS_CLIENT` token |
| `RequestWithUser` defined twice | `auth.controller.ts` + `common/types.ts` | Delete local copy, import from common |
| `findShareOrThrow` pattern 6x | `shares.service.ts` | Extract private helper |
| `uint8ToBase64` duplicated | `folder.service.ts` + `file-metadata.service.ts` | Move to `utils/encoding.ts` |
| `truncatePublicKey`/`truncatePubkey` | `ShareDialog.tsx` + `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` | Add to existing `utils/format.ts` |
| `MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH = 20` defined 3x | `folder.service.ts`, `useFolder.ts`, `MoveDialog.tsx` | Single export from folder.service |
| `InitVaultDto` hand-written alongside generated | `lib/api/vault.ts` vs `api/models/` | Delete hand-written, use generated |
| REQUIRED_SHARE block 3x in `useAuth` | `loginWithGoogle`, `loginWithEmail`, `loginWithWallet` | Extract `handleRequiredShare()` helper |
| Controller has repo access + business logic | `identity.controller.ts` | Move `findOrCreateUserByIdentifier` to service |
| `publishBatch` duplicates single-record logic | `ipns.service.ts` | Extract `processSingleRecord` helper |
| Inline IPFS fetch+decode 4x | `useSharedNavigation.ts` | Reuse existing `fetchAndDecryptMetadata` |
| Catch variable inconsistency | 206 catch blocks use `err`/`error`/`e` randomly | Pick one, add ESLint rule |
| Metrics 100-line constructor | `metrics.service.ts` | Extract `initializeMetrics()` method |

---

### Architecture Observations (Not Bugs, But Worth Noting)

- **Desktop `auth.ts` (771 lines) parallels web `useAuth.ts` (510 lines)** — structural duplication from the Tauri/browser split. Can't easily share code but worth keeping aligned.
- **12 services call `useStore.getState()` directly** — valid Zustand pattern but creates implicit coupling. No circular deps detected.
- **`generate-openapi.ts` has 39 manual imports** — must be updated every time a controller is added. Consider auto-discovery.
- **Rust IPNS implementation (408 lines of hand-rolled CBOR/protobuf)** parallels the TypeScript `ipns` npm package — works but risks silent divergence if the spec changes.

Want me to start implementing any of these? I'd suggest starting with Tier 1 items since they're the highest-ROI with the least risk.

   106→function isTextFile(name: string): boolean {
   107→  const lower = name.toLowerCase();

   109→  if (TEXT_EXTENSIONS.has(lower)) return true;
   110→  // Handle well-known extensionless filenames like Dockerfile, Makefile
   111→  if (TEXT_FILENAMES.has(lower)) return true;

   113→  if (lastDot === -1) return false;
   114→  return TEXT_EXTENSIONS.has(lower.slice(lastDot));
   115→}

   178→
   179→/**

   181→ */
   182→function isPreviewableFile(name: string): boolean {
   183→  return isImageFile(name) || isPdfFile(name) || isAudioFile(name) || isVideoFile(name);
   184→}

   194→/**
   195→ * Main file browser container component.
   196→ *
   197→ * Manages the file list area with in-place navigation.
   198→ * Selection state, context menu, and file/folder actions.
   199→ *
   200→ * Navigation:
   201→ * - Double-click folders to navigate into them
   202→ * - Click [..] PARENT_DIR row to navigate up

   204→ *

   207→ * - Rename: Opens dialog to rename file/folder
   208→ * - Delete: Shows confirmation, then removes from folder metadata
   209→ * - Move: Drag-drop to folder rows in list
   210→ *
   211→ * @example

   215→ *     <main className="app-main">
   216→ *       <FileBrowser />
   217→ *     </main>
   218→ *   );
   219→ * }
   220→ * ```
   221→ */

   227→

   230→    createFolder,

   232→    moveItem,
   233→    moveItems,
   234→    deleteItem,
   235→    deleteItems,
   236→    isLoading: isOperating,
   237→  } = useFolder();

   247→
   248→  // Vault and folder stores for sync
   249→  const { rootIpnsName } = useVaultStore();
   250→  const initialSyncComplete = useSyncStore((state) => state.initialSyncComplete);
   251→  const syncStatus = useSyncStore((state) => state.status);
   252→
   253→  // Sync callback - compare remote sequence with local, refresh if different
   254→  const handleSync = useCallback(async () => {
   255→    if (!rootIpnsName) return;
   256→
   257→    // Resolve root folder IPNS to get remote CID and sequence number
   258→    // Note: resolveIpnsRecord returns null when the record definitively
   259→    // doesn't exist (backend 404 / success:false). Transient failures
   260→    // (network errors, 5xx) throw instead, which useSyncPolling catches.
   261→    const resolved = await resolveIpnsRecord(rootIpnsName);
   262→    if (!resolved) {
   263→      // No IPNS record found. This is expected for vaults that have
   264→      // never published (empty vault, no uploads yet). Return normally
   265→      // so useSyncPolling marks initial sync complete and shows the
   266→      // empty file browser instead of retrying forever.
   267→      return;
   268→    }
   269→
   270→    // Read fresh state from store — avoid stale closure from render cycle.
   271→    // On initial load, the root folder may not be in the closure's `folders`
   272→    // yet because useFolderNavigation's useEffect sets it in the same commit.
   273→    const rootFolder = useFolderStore.getState().folders['root'];
   274→    if (!rootFolder) return;
   275→
   276→    // Compare sequence numbers - if remote > local, we need to refresh
   277→    // Sequence number comparison is more reliable than CID since we
   278→    // don't cache the local CID, and sequence always increments
   279→    if (resolved.sequenceNumber <= rootFolder.sequenceNumber) {
   280→      // No changes, already up to date
   281→      return;
   282→    }
   283→
   284→    // Remote has newer version - fetch and decrypt new metadata

     1→import {
     2→  Injectable,
     3→  HttpException,

     9→} from '@nestjs/common';
    10→import { InjectRepository } from '@nestjs/typeorm';
    11→import { Repository } from 'typeorm';
    12→import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';
    13→import { FolderIpns } from './entities/folder-ipns.entity';
    14→import {
    15→  PublishIpnsDto,
    16→  PublishIpnsResponseDto,
    17→  BatchPublishIpnsDto,
    18→  BatchPublishIpnsResponseDto,
    19→} from './dto';
    20→import { RepublishService } from '../republish/republish.service';
    21→import { parseIpnsRecord } from './ipns-record-parser';
    22→
    23→@Injectable()
    24→export class IpnsService {
    25→  private readonly logger = new Logger(IpnsService.name);
    26→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    27→  private readonly maxRetries = 3;
    28→  private readonly baseDelayMs = 1000;
    29→
    30→  constructor(
    31→    @InjectRepository(FolderIpns)
    32→    private readonly folderIpnsRepository: Repository<FolderIpns>,
    33→    private readonly configService: ConfigService,
    34→    @Inject(forwardRef(() => RepublishService))
    35→    private readonly republishService: RepublishService
    36→  ) {
    37→    this.delegatedRoutingUrl = this.configService.get<string>(
    38→      'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',
    39→      'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'
    40→    );
    41→  }
    42→
    43→  /**
    44→   * Publish a pre-signed IPNS record to the IPFS network via delegated routing
    45→   * and track the folder in the database for TEE republishing
    46→   */
    47→  async publishRecord(userId: string, dto: PublishIpnsDto): Promise<PublishIpnsResponseDto> {
    48→    // Validate base64 record
    49→    let recordBytes: Uint8Array;
    50→    try {
    51→      recordBytes = Uint8Array.from(atob(dto.record), (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));
    52→    } catch {
    53→      throw new BadRequestException('Invalid base64-encoded record');
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    // Save to DB first so resolve always has a fallback, even if delegated
    57→    // routing fails (e.g. rate-limited, network error, DHT propagation delay).
    58→    const folder = await this.upsertFolderIpns(
    59→      userId,
    60→      dto.ipnsName,
    61→      dto.metadataCid,
    62→      dto.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
    63→      dto.keyEpoch
    64→    );
    65→
    66→    // Publish to delegated routing API (best-effort — DB is the reliable source)
    67→    try {
    68→      await this.publishToDelegatedRouting(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes);
    69→    } catch (error) {
    70→      this.logger.warn(
    71→        `Delegated routing publish failed for ${dto.ipnsName}, DB record saved: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
    72→      );
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    return {
    76→      success: true,
    77→      ipnsName: dto.ipnsName,
    78→      sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
    79→    };
    80→  }
    81→
    82→  /**
    83→   * Batch publish multiple IPNS records with concurrency-limited processing.
    84→   * Supports partial success: individual record failures do not fail the batch.
    85→   * Processes up to 10 records concurrently.
    86→   */
    87→  async publishBatch(
    88→    userId: string,
    89→    dto: BatchPublishIpnsDto
    90→  ): Promise<BatchPublishIpnsResponseDto> {
    91→    const results: PublishIpnsResponseDto[] = [];
    92→    let totalSucceeded = 0;
    93→    let totalFailed = 0;
    94→
    95→    const CONCURRENCY = 10;
    96→
    97→    // Process records in batches of CONCURRENCY
    98→    for (let i = 0; i < dto.records.length; i += CONCURRENCY) {
    99→      const batch = dto.records.slice(i, i + CONCURRENCY);
   100→
   101→      const settled = await Promise.allSettled(
   102→        batch.map(async (entry) => {
   103→          // Validate base64 record
   104→          let recordBytes: Uint8Array;
   105→          try {
   106→            recordBytes = Uint8Array.from(atob(entry.record), (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));
   107→          } catch {
   108→            throw new BadRequestException(`Invalid base64-encoded record for ${entry.ipnsName}`);
   109→          }
   110→
   111→          // Save to DB first so resolve always has a fallback
   112→          const folder = await this.upsertFolderIpns(
   113→            userId,
   114→            entry.ipnsName,
   115→            entry.metadataCid,
   116→            entry.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
   117→            entry.keyEpoch,
   118→            entry.recordType ?? 'folder'
   119→          );
   120→
   121→          // Publish to delegated routing (best-effort)
   122→          try {
   123→            await this.publishToDelegatedRouting(entry.ipnsName, recordBytes);
   124→          } catch (error) {
   125→            this.logger.warn(
   126→              `Delegated routing publish failed for ${entry.ipnsName}, DB record saved: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
   127→            );
   128→          }
   129→
   130→          return {
   131→            success: true,
   132→            ipnsName: entry.ipnsName,
   133→            sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
   134→          } as PublishIpnsResponseDto;
   135→        })
   136→      );
   137→
   138→      for (let j = 0; j < settled.length; j++) {
   139→        const result = settled[j];
   140→        if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
   141→          results.push(result.value);
   142→          totalSucceeded++;
   143→        } else {
   144→          const reason = result.reason;
   145→          const ipnsName = batch[j]?.ipnsName ?? 'unknown';
   146→          this.logger.warn(
   147→            `Batch publish failed for ${ipnsName}: ${reason instanceof Error ? reason.message : String(reason)}`
   148→          );
   149→          results.push({
   150→            success: false,
   151→            ipnsName,
   152→            sequenceNumber: '0',
   153→          });
   154→          totalFailed++;
   155→        }
   156→      }
   157→    }
   158→
   159→    return { results, totalSucceeded, totalFailed };
   160→  }
   161→
   162→  /**
   163→   * Publish record to delegated routing API with exponential backoff retry
   164→   */
   165→  private async publishToDelegatedRouting(
   166→    ipnsName: string,
   167→    recordBytes: Uint8Array
   168→  ): Promise<void> {
   169→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${ipnsName}`;
   170→
   171→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
   172→
   173→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
   174→      try {
   175→        const response = await fetch(url, {
   176→          method: 'PUT',
   177→          headers: {
   178→            'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
   179→          },
   180→          body: recordBytes as unknown as BodyInit,
   181→        });
   182→
   183→        if (response.ok) {
   184→          this.logger.log(`IPNS record published successfully for ${ipnsName}`);
   185→          return;
   186→        }
   187→
   188→        // Handle rate limiting
   189→        if (response.status === 429) {
   190→          const retryAfter = response.headers.get('Retry-After');
   191→          const delayMs = retryAfter
   192→            ? parseInt(retryAfter, 10) * 1000
   193→            : this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   194→
   195→          this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS publish, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   196→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   197→          continue;
   198→        }
   199→
   200→        // Non-retryable error
   201→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   202→        const errorText = await response.text();
   203→        this.logger.error(
   204→          `Delegated routing returned ${response.status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   205→        );
   206→        throw new Error(`Delegated routing returned ${response.status}`);
   207→      } catch (error) {
   208→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   209→
   210→        // Only retry on network errors, not on HTTP errors
   211→        if (
   212→          lastError.message.includes('Delegated routing returned') &&
   213→          !lastError.message.includes('429')
   214→        ) {
   215→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   216→          throw new HttpException(
   217→            'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network',
   218→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   219→          );
   220→        }
   221→
   222→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   223→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   224→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   225→          this.logger.warn(
   226→            `IPNS publish attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
   227→          );
   228→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   229→        }
   230→      }
   231→    }
   232→
   233→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   234→    this.logger.error(
   235→      `Failed to publish IPNS record after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   236→    );
   237→    throw new HttpException(
   238→      'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network after multiple attempts',
   239→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   240→    );
   241→  }
   242→
   243→  /**
   244→   * Create or update a folder/file IPNS entry.
   245→   * Handles both folder metadata and per-file metadata IPNS records.
   246→   */
   247→  private async upsertFolderIpns(
   248→    userId: string,
   249→    ipnsName: string,
   250→    metadataCid: string,
   251→    encryptedIpnsPrivateKey?: string,
   252→    keyEpoch?: number,
   253→    recordType: 'folder' | 'file' = 'folder'
   254→  ): Promise<FolderIpns> {
   255→    const existing = await this.getFolderIpns(userId, ipnsName);
   256→
   257→    if (existing) {
   258→      // Update existing entry
   259→      existing.latestCid = metadataCid;
   260→      existing.sequenceNumber = (BigInt(existing.sequenceNumber) + 1n).toString();
   261→      existing.recordType = recordType;
   262→      existing.updatedAt = new Date();
   263→
   264→      // Only update encrypted key if provided (e.g., on key rotation)
   265→      if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   266→        existing.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey = Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex');
   267→        existing.keyEpoch = keyEpoch;
   268→      }
   269→
   270→      const saved = await this.folderIpnsRepository.save(existing);
   271→
   272→      // Auto-enroll for TEE republishing when encrypted key is provided
   273→      if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   274→        this.republishService
   275→          .enrollFolder(
   276→            userId,
   277→            ipnsName,
   278→            Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex'),
   279→            keyEpoch,
   280→            metadataCid,
   281→            saved.sequenceNumber
   282→          )
   283→          .catch((err) =>
   284→            this.logger.warn(
   285→              `Failed to enroll ${recordType} ${ipnsName} for republishing: ${err.message}`
   286→            )
   287→          );
   288→      }
   289→
   290→      return saved;
   291→    }
   292→
   293→    // Create new entry
   294→    const folder = this.folderIpnsRepository.create({
   295→      userId,
   296→      ipnsName,
   297→      latestCid: metadataCid,
   298→      sequenceNumber: '0',
   299→      encryptedIpnsPrivateKey: encryptedIpnsPrivateKey
   300→        ? Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex')
   301→        : null,
   302→      keyEpoch: keyEpoch ?? null,
   303→      isRoot: false, // Root folder is tracked in Vault entity
   304→      recordType,
   305→    });
   306→
   307→    const saved = await this.folderIpnsRepository.save(folder);
   308→
   309→    // Auto-enroll for TEE republishing when encrypted key is provided
   310→    if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   311→      this.republishService
   312→        .enrollFolder(
   313→          userId,
   314→          ipnsName,
   315→          Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex'),
   316→          keyEpoch,
   317→          metadataCid,
   318→          saved.sequenceNumber
   319→        )
   320→        .catch((err) =>
   321→          this.logger.warn(
   322→            `Failed to enroll ${recordType} ${ipnsName} for republishing: ${err.message}`
   323→          )
   324→        );
   325→    }
   326→
   327→    return saved;
   328→  }
   329→
   330→  /**
   331→   * Get a folder IPNS entry by user and IPNS name
   332→   */
   333→  async getFolderIpns(userId: string, ipnsName: string): Promise<FolderIpns | null> {
   334→    return this.folderIpnsRepository.findOne({
   335→      where: { userId, ipnsName },
   336→    });
   337→  }
   338→
   339→  /**
   340→   * Get all folder IPNS entries for a user (for TEE republishing)
   341→   */
   342→  async getAllFolderIpns(userId: string): Promise<FolderIpns[]> {
   343→    return this.folderIpnsRepository.find({
   344→      where: { userId },
   345→      order: { createdAt: 'ASC' },
   346→    });
   347→  }
   348→
   349→  /**
   350→   * Resolve an IPNS name to its current CID via delegated routing,
   351→   * falling back to the DB-cached CID when delegated routing is unavailable
   352→   * or when the record is not found in the DHT.
   353→   * Returns null if the IPNS name is not found anywhere (404)
   354→   */
   355→  async resolveRecord(ipnsName: string): Promise<{
   356→    cid: string;
   357→    sequenceNumber: string;
   358→    signatureV2?: string;
   359→    data?: string;
   360→    pubKey?: string;
   361→  } | null> {
   362→    let result: {
   363→      cid: string;
   364→      sequenceNumber: string;
   365→      signatureV2?: string;
   366→      data?: string;
   367→      pubKey?: string;
   368→    } | null = null;
   369→
   370→    try {
   371→      result = await this.resolveFromDelegatedRouting(ipnsName);
   372→    } catch (error) {
   373→      // Fall back to DB cache on BAD_GATEWAY (delegated routing failures)
   374→      if (error instanceof HttpException && error.getStatus() === HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY) {
   375→        this.logger.warn(`Delegated routing failed for ${ipnsName}, falling back to DB cache`);
   376→      } else {
   377→        throw error;
   378→      }
   379→    }
   380→
   381→    // Always check DB cache — it's written synchronously during publish
   382→    // and may be ahead of the network (delegated routing can serve stale records)
   383→    const cached = await this.folderIpnsRepository.findOne({
   384→      where: { ipnsName },
   385→    });
   386→
   387→    if (result && cached?.latestCid) {
   388→      // Both sources available — prefer the one with the higher sequence number
   389→      const networkSeq = BigInt(result.sequenceNumber);
   390→      const dbSeq = BigInt(cached.sequenceNumber);
   391→      if (dbSeq > networkSeq) {
   392→        this.logger.log(
   393→          `DB cache has newer sequence (${dbSeq} > ${networkSeq}) for ${ipnsName}, using DB: ${cached.latestCid}`
   394→        );
   395→        return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };
   396→      }
   397→      return result;
   398→    }
   399→
   400→    if (result) {
   401→      return result;
   402→    }
   403→
   404→    // Delegated routing returned null (404) or threw BAD_GATEWAY — try DB cache
   405→    if (cached?.latestCid) {
   406→      this.logger.log(`Resolved ${ipnsName} from DB cache: ${cached.latestCid}`);
   407→      return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };
   408→    }
   409→
   410→    return null;
   411→  }
   412→
   413→  /**
   414→   * Resolve an IPNS name via the delegated routing API with retries.
   415→   * Returns null if the IPNS name is not found (404).
   416→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on routing failures.
   417→   */
   418→  private async resolveFromDelegatedRouting(ipnsName: string): Promise<{
   419→    cid: string;
   420→    sequenceNumber: string;
   421→    signatureV2?: string;
   422→    data?: string;
   423→    pubKey?: string;
   424→  } | null> {
   425→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${ipnsName}`;
   426→
   427→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
   428→
   429→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
   430→      try {
   431→        const response = await fetch(url, {
   432→          method: 'GET',
   433→          headers: {
   434→            Accept: 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
   435→          },
   436→        });
   437→
   438→        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error
   439→        if (response.status === 404) {
   440→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found: ${ipnsName}`);
   441→          return null;
   442→        }
   443→
   444→        if (response.ok) {
   445→          // The delegated routing API returns the raw IPNS record
   446→          // We need to parse it to extract the CID and sequence number
   447→          const recordBytes = new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer());
   448→          const parsed = this.parseIpnsRecordBytes(recordBytes);
   449→
   450→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name resolved successfully: ${ipnsName} -> ${parsed.cid}`);
   451→          return parsed;
   452→        }
   453→
   454→        // Handle rate limiting
   455→        if (response.status === 429) {
   456→          const retryAfter = response.headers.get('Retry-After');
   457→          const delayMs = retryAfter
   458→            ? parseInt(retryAfter, 10) * 1000
   459→            : this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   460→
   461→          this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS resolve, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   462→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   463→          continue;
   464→        }
   465→
   466→        // Non-retryable error
   467→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   468→        const errorText = await response.text();
   469→        this.logger.error(
   470→          `Delegated routing resolution returned ${response.status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   471→        );
   472→        throw new Error(`Delegated routing returned ${response.status}`);
   473→      } catch (error) {
   474→        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry
   475→        if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   476→          throw error;
   477→        }
   478→
   479→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   480→
   481→        // Only retry on network errors, not on HTTP errors
   482→        if (
   483→          lastError.message.includes('Delegated routing returned') &&
   484→          !lastError.message.includes('429')
   485→        ) {
   486→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   487→          throw new HttpException(
   488→            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',
   489→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   490→          );
   491→        }
   492→
   493→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   494→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   495→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   496→          this.logger.warn(
   497→            `IPNS resolve attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
   498→          );
   499→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   500→        }
   501→      }
   502→    }
   503→
   504→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   505→    this.logger.error(
   506→      `Failed to resolve IPNS name after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   507→    );
   508→    throw new HttpException(
   509→      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',
   510→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   511→    );
   512→  }
   513→
   514→  /**
   515→   * Parse an IPNS record to extract CID and sequence number
   516→   * Uses inline protobuf decoder — no external dependencies
   517→   */
   518→  private parseIpnsRecordBytes(recordBytes: Uint8Array): {
   519→    cid: string;
   520→    sequenceNumber: string;
   521→    signatureV2?: string;
   522→    data?: string;
   523→    pubKey?: string;
   524→  } {
   525→    try {
   526→      const record = parseIpnsRecord(recordBytes);
   527→
   528→      // Extract CID from the Value field (format: /ipfs/<cid>)
   529→      const valuePath = record.value;
   530→      const cidMatch = valuePath.match(/\/ipfs\/([a-zA-Z0-9]+)/);
   531→      if (!cidMatch) {
   532→        this.logger.error('Failed to extract CID from IPNS record value');
   533→        throw new HttpException('Invalid IPNS record format', HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);
   534→      }
   535→
   536→      const cid = cidMatch[1];
   537→      const sequenceNumber = String(record.sequence ?? 0n);
   538→
   539→      // Base64-encode signature fields if present
   540→      const signatureV2 = record.signatureV2
   541→        ? Buffer.from(record.signatureV2).toString('base64')
   542→        : undefined;
   543→      const data = record.data ? Buffer.from(record.data).toString('base64') : undefined;
   544→      const pubKey = record.pubKey ? Buffer.from(record.pubKey).toString('base64') : undefined;
   545→
   546→      this.logger.debug(`Parsed IPNS record: cid=${cid}, sequenceNumber=${sequenceNumber}`);
   547→      return { cid, sequenceNumber, signatureV2, data, pubKey };
   548→    } catch (error) {
   549→      if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   550→        throw error;
   551→      }
   552→      this.logger.error(`Failed to parse IPNS record: ${error}`);
   553→      throw new HttpException('Invalid IPNS record format', HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);
   554→    }
   555→  }
   556→
   557→  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
   558→    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
   559→  }
   560→}
   561→

    25→@Injectable()
    26→export class RepublishService {
    27→  private readonly logger = new Logger(RepublishService.name);
    28→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    29→  private readonly maxPublishRetries = 3;
    30→  private readonly publishBaseDelayMs = 1000;
    31→
    32→  constructor(
    33→    @InjectRepository(IpnsRepublishSchedule)
    34→    private readonly scheduleRepository: Repository<IpnsRepublishSchedule>,
    35→    @InjectRepository(FolderIpns)
    36→    private readonly folderIpnsRepository: Repository<FolderIpns>,
